# Tankbook – Task Backlog

*`PHASES.md` broken into agent-sized tasks. Each task names its deliverable and the **checks that make it done** – a task without its checks green is not done, and checks land in the same PR as the code (TESTING.md levels: L1 unit · L2 contract · L3 sync-scenario · L4 UI/snapshot · L5 accuracy · M manual). Task IDs are stable – reference them in branches/PRs (`p0-4-grdb-migrations`).*

## Implied in every task's checks: it builds and it lints

The per-task checks below are **in addition to** the baseline gate, which is never restated per row because it applies to all of them: every tier a task touches must compile and its linter must exit **0** (`CLAUDE.md` hard rule 14, `docs/TESTING.md` → the baseline gate). Run `swiftlint lint` **from the repo root**, and judge it by exit code. A task whose feature tests pass but which leaves the build broken or the linter red is **not** done and does not get an `[x]`.

## Status legend

`[x]` done and independently verified – its own checks **and** the baseline gate re-run outside the implementing agent · `[~]` partially landed, gap named · `[ ]` not started · `[!]` **blocked on an action only the product owner can take** (a purchase, a legal decision, an account) - not work an agent can pick up · `[cut]` **decided against, with the reason recorded in the row and in the owning doc** – a cut task is not work waiting to be picked up, and re-proposing it needs the recorded reason to have changed. Status is recorded here so a fresh session knows what to pick up without re-deriving it.

## Version scope

*`CLAUDE.md` → Version scope is the convention. **A row without a marker is v1** – the launch build owes it, and the launch triage below says in which tier. Rows marked **[v1.0.x]**, **[v1.1]**, **[v1.x]** or **[v2]** are deferred to that release; the marker repeats the triage's target so a row can be read on its own. Section AG is v2 in its entirety.*

## Launch triage (2026-08-29) – what v1 needs, what waits

*Every open row (P-, W-, PR-, PJ-) sorted against one question: **can the app be submitted and
used by a stranger without this?** Three tiers. **Blocker** – submission is impossible or the
first session breaks a hard rule visibly. **Required** – a v1 promise (`VISION.md` MVP row, a
journey "always/never", or a written security/privacy commitment) that the code does not keep;
ship without it only with a written product-owner exception. **Deferred** – real, tracked, and
not a v1 condition; target named. Sizes are relative (S under a day of agent work, M a few days,
L a week-plus). Ordering inside a tier is the recommended sequence.*

**Two decisions this triage assumes, each reversible by the product owner:**

1. **The backend ships with v1.** `api.tankbook.live` is deliberately 502 today. Without it the
   app loses not only sync and cloud extract but **import parsing** (server-side by rule 9),
   **fresh exchange rates** (the seed pack ages from day one), remote config and the `appUpdate`
   notice – three MVP rows and the only way to retire a bad build. The backend is built and
   L2/L3-tested (P4, P5 complete); what is missing is the deployment itself, which no row tracks.
2. **v1 ships what is built, not only VISION's MVP column.** Service, reminders and sync are
   "v1.x" in `VISION.md` but P3 and P4 are complete; they ship, so their unreachable or
   half-wired parts count as required. EV charging (J6) and the Pro tier stay out.

### The v1.1 priority queue (product owner, 2026-08-31)

Eleven rows pulled out of the v1.1 bucket and **ordered**. Version markers are unchanged - these
stay `[v1.1]`/`[v1.x]` - this is a sequencing decision, not a scope change. Rows are marked
**PRIORITY** in place.

**Why these eleven.** Seven are the J7 cluster: P3 built the nouns (service entries, parts, tire
sets, reminders) and skipped most of the verbs that connect them, which is how the phase reads
COMPLETE while the journey does not hold together. Four are already-built machinery that nothing
calls - the cheapest rows in the backlog.

| # | Task | What the user hits today |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **PJ.28** | **Expense capture photographs the receipt and discards it.** The shutter behaves normally; `ExpenseEntryView` saves `attachments: []`. Nothing is shown, nothing is kept - the only row here that silently loses data |
| 2 | **PJ.22** | **The service log never proposes the next service.** No lifetime editor, so `proposedReminderId` is always nil. J7's promise is "the app proposes the next reminder"; today you log the past and remember the future yourself |
| 3 | **PJ.24** | **One door at completion.** The ReminderComplete sheet offers Type and Skip while the invoice is in the user's hand - hard rule 15 says the camera is a peer at every entry point |
| 4 | **PJ.23** | **A service line is frozen once saved.** Title, category and cost are uneditable, so a typo means delete and re-enter, losing attachments and links. `.other` "promoted later without data loss" has no promotion path |
| 5 | **PJ.26** | **Tires and their price never meet.** `purchaseExpenseId` exists in the schema and migration and is written **nowhere** - a tire set cannot say what it cost |
| 6 | **PJ.27** | **Nothing reminds you to swap back in spring.** A mount is recorded, then silence for six months |
| 7 | **PJ.25** | **The parts shelf is unreachable from the Garage** - it works, but only opens from inside a service entry |
| 8 | **PJ.19** | Station suggestion: the logic is **written** in `JOURNEYS.md`/`SCHEMA.md` and never called |
| 9 | **PJ.34** | F9a already ranks suggestions; the UI does not consume them, so receipt/QR date priority is computed and discarded |
| 10 | **PJ.35** | `.blobPrefetch` is a `PowerWorkKind` case **nothing produces** (P6.20) - photos never prefetch |
| 11 | **PJ.45** | `paceLimitKmPerDay` is a real per-vehicle field **edited nowhere in the app** |

Rows 8-11 are the pattern worth watching: **the mechanism exists, tested, and no user can reach
it.** Ask what produces a case, not only what handles it - the same question that produced P6.20.

### SH · Ship tasks (no row covered these)

| ID | Task | Checks (done when...) |
|---|---|---|
| **[~]** SH.1 | **DEPLOYED AND VERIFIED 2026-09-02, two clauses outstanding.** `https://api.tankbook.live` is live behind an external nginx: TLS valid, HTTP/2, HSTS/nosniff/referrer-policy confirmed **on the wire** (not read off the config - that is how the site shipped without them). Postgres provisioned by `backend/scripts/provision-database.sql`, 12 migrations applied, `/v1/catalog` and `/v1/config` serving. **The config signing key IS provisioned** - the live document verifies against the RELEASE bundled key using the app's own verifier, so remote config can govern a shipped build; this also closes the standing "signature fails open to bundled defaults" item. **S3 works end to end**: `/import/parse` (the one S3 path needing no bearer) wrote `{deviceId}/imports/{id}/original` and `result.json` into `tankbook-blobs` and read the parse back (200). Secrets absent from every tracked file; `appsettings*.json` still gitignored. **Outstanding: (1) a real Apple sign-in creating an account and round-tripping push/pull - needs a real Apple id token, so it belongs to `SH.3` on the floor device; (2) `/rates` answers correctly but returns `quotes: []` - the endpoint is right, the feed has not populated, so the rates job or its source needs a look.** Also unmet as written: "from a phone on cellular" and "on a device" - both checked from a workstation | `GET /health` 200 over TLS **[done]**; `GET /v1/config` verifies against the bundled key **[done, release arm, not on a device]**; a real Apple sign-in creates an account and a push/pull round-trips **[NOT DONE -> SH.3]**; `/rates` serves yesterday's date **[partial: correct shape, empty quotes]**; `/import/parse` round-trips the MFM fixture **[done]**; secrets absent from every committed file **[done]** |
| **[~]** SH.2 | **The build path is scripted (2026-08-30, unverified against a real team):** `scripts/release.sh` archives (`generic/platform=iOS`, `-allowProvisioningUpdates`), exports with `ios/App/ExportOptions.plist` (`app-store-connect`, automatic signing, symbols) and uploads with `xcrun altool` and an App Store Connect API key from the environment; the build number is the commit count and a dirty tree refuses to archive. `project.yml` carries `CODE_SIGN_STYLE: Automatic`, `DEVELOPMENT_TEAM: ${TANKBOOK_TEAM_ID}` (an env var - unset, simulator builds still work), `MARKETING_VERSION` / `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION` feeding the plist, and `ios/App/Tankbook.entitlements` with Sign in with Apple. Simulator build green with the new plist wiring (`1.0.0` / `1`). **What remains needs the owner's team id and a registered App ID: the first real `scripts/release.sh` run, the TestFlight install on the floor device, and the CI re-enable.** The checklist is `docs/STORE.md` → Release checklist. **Release build path**: `xcodebuild archive` + export from a clean checkout on one documented machine, bundle version `1.0.0`+build; iOS CI re-enabled with `swift test` split from the UI suite and a path filter (HANDOVER names both causes); the release checklist in `docs/STORE.md` lists the two owner declarations from P6.6 | A TestFlight build installs on the iPhone 12 / iOS 18 floor device and the M-checklist passes; `gh workflow enable "iOS Core"` and one green run |
| **[ ]** SH.3 | **Launch-readiness walk** on the TestFlight build, by a human, on the floor device: J1 (fresh install to first logged entry under 3 min, typed and scanned), J3 five live fill-ups (P2's M-check), J2 with the real MFM export, J10 one foreign receipt, J11a sign-in on a second device, F3 airplane mode through a full capture, F8 camera denied. Every screenshot EN and RU | Each journey's success metric recorded in `docs/STORE.md` with the build number; any failure files a task before submission |
| **[x]** SH.4 | **DONE 2026-09-01, verified.** Google is **wired, without the SDK** (product owner: a third-party SDK and its transitive dependencies are not worth one button). OAuth 2.0 authorization-code + PKCE through `ASWebAuthenticationSession`: `GoogleOAuth` (core, pure, 25 L1 tests) builds the request, validates the callback and shapes the exchange; `GoogleWebAuthenticator` (app) presents the browser and runs the POST on a bare `URLSession` - NOT `TankbookHTTPClient`, which rightly refuses `oauth2.googleapis.com` and must carry no Tankbook bearer. **The dead control is gone by construction**: the button renders exactly when `GoogleOAuth.Configuration.provisioned` returns non-nil, so a build with no client id offers Apple alone - measured, a Release build ships `GoogleClientID` empty. The J11a wrong-provider question is gated the same way. Mutation-checked: the `code_challenge` reverted to the verifier, and the `state` check relaxed to presence-only, each fail their test. **It also found and fixed a live account-takeover vector** - see the new `PR.35` row. Commit at merge | L4 `SignInUITests` 7/7 on `iPhone 17`; unit 1152; screenshots `SH.4-sign-in-google-{en,ru}.png` opened in EN and RU |
| **[x]** PR.35 | **DONE 2026-09-01, verified. Found while wiring SH.4.** `AppleGoogleIdTokenVerifier` verified an identity token's **signature** and never its `aud` or `iss` - and Apple's and Google's JWKS sign tokens for *every* client on their platforms. So any developer shipping an app with Apple or Google sign-in could collect their own users' id tokens and replay them at `POST /auth/session` to take over the matching Tankbook account. Live for Apple today, independent of Google. Now checked per provider against `Auth:AppleAudiences` / `Auth:GoogleAudiences` before any other claim is read, and it **fails closed**: an unconfigured allowlist refuses every token rather than accepting any. `Auth:Audience` is a different thing (the audience this server *stamps*) and `docs/STORE.md` §4.3 said to set it for this purpose - that sentence is corrected. **The class had no tests at all**: every L2 endpoint test injects `TestIdTokenSigner.Verifier`, a reimplementation that checks the signature and the expiry and nothing else. **Blocks `SH.1`** - the audiences must be set at deploy or every sign-in refuses | 14 new L2 tests against the REAL verifier; mutation-checked twice (fail-open on an empty allowlist, and dropping the per-provider split) each failing exactly its own test; backend 295, `dotnet format` 0 |

L2: push rejects oversize/malformed/schema-violating payloads with the right code and JSON pointer; unknown entityType accepted unvalidated; `minSupported` gate returns 426 on push while pull still succeeds. L1+L2 parity: Swift upcaster and server declarative transform produce byte-identical output for every fixture. Round-trip: unknown fields and unknown entity types survive decode→encode unchanged |

No step may read a decimal count or a position. | **[x]** P2.14 | **`FuelExtractor.modal` is non-deterministic, and it destabilises every accuracy number in the project.** The total-finder resolves ties with `Dictionary(grouping:)`, whose iteration order Swift does not guarantee, and then `modes.max(by:)` on equal `primaryCounts` returns whichever element that order left last - with `?? modes.first` order-dependent too. So when two total candidates tie on count **and** on primary-label count, the answer is whatever the hash seed decided. Found by P4.13, which required three sweeps per image and watched the receipt score flip between **29/96 and 30/96** across identical runs (`receipt-021`, `-026`, `-029`). **This is not only a parser bug: `high-water.json` and `PumpPhotoGate.measuredHits` are gates asserted against a live score, so a tie-break that moves makes CI itself intermittently red - and it means the receipts mark cannot safely be raised from its stale 45/93 to the live 46/96 until this lands.** The fix must be deterministic **and the tie-break must be chosen deliberately and stated**, not just stabilised: the strongest candidate is to **return `nil`**, because the parser already refuses rather than guesses elsewhere (a printed `0.00` becomes `nil`, an unmarked operand pair returns `nil`) and hard rule 13 says a confident wrong value is worse than an empty field the user fills. If refusing lowers the score, that is **information, not failure** | L1: a fixture whose totals tie on both counts returns the **same** answer across 100 runs **in separate processes** - one process shares a hash seed and would pass while the bug is live; the chosen tie-break is asserted **by name** (nil, or the stated rule), not merely 'stable'; `receipt-021`/`-026`/`-029` are asserted individually. L5: re-score after the fix and **raise the receipts high-water mark in the same change**, with the new number proven stable across repeated runs. **Vacuous traps**: asserting determinism by calling the function twice in one process (same seed, always agrees); asserting 'a value is returned' rather than which; fixing `modal` alone and leaving `?? modes.first` order-dependent |

| **[cut]** P2.8 | ~~Foundation Models normalization layer (iOS 26+ gated)~~ **Cut 2026-08-25, not deferred.** The on-device model reports itself `unavailable` on an unsupported device language - the shipped iOS 26.5 SDK carries `SystemLanguageModel.UnavailableReason.unsupportedLanguageOrLocale` next to `supportedLanguages` - and **Russian is not in the supported set** (nor Kazakh). The corpus is 32 RU/KZ receipts and receipts score 41.6%, so the layer would have been unavailable exactly where the rules parser is weakest and available only where it already does best. The task's own check (*ships only if it strictly improves on an A/B against the corpus*) is therefore unsatisfiable in principle, not merely unmeasured - a stronger reason to stop than P2.7's 0/30. **Not blocked on the EU**, which has had Apple Intelligence since iOS 18.4 (April 2025); the constraint is language, not jurisdiction. Reasoning and the re-open condition (Apple adding Russian to `supportedLanguages`) live in `docs/VISION.md` -> "Why tier 2 was cut". Normalization for Russian receipts is now tier 3's job (the cloud LLM gateway) | **No code shipped, by decision.** The measurement that would have gated it cannot be taken |

| **[x]** P3.7 | **The tab bar double-counted the home indicator.** `AppTabBar.bottomPadding` is `28`, taken from `HomeA.dc.html`'s `padding: 10px 12px 28px` - but the artboard is a browser page with **no safe area**, so that 28 px *is* the designer's home-indicator allowance. The app then draws the bar through `safeAreaInset(edge: .bottom)`, which adds the real inset (~34 pt on iPhone 17) underneath it, leaving **~62 pt of dead space below the labels against the artboard's 28**. Spend the artboard's allowance **through** the safe area instead: `max(8, 28 - safeAreaInsets.bottom)` - ~42 pt on an indicator device, exactly 28 on an SE, and the 8 pt floor stops the labels touching the indicator on tall devices (the opposite mistake). `contentHeight` is derived from this padding, so `TabRoots.toastBottomClearance` and `contentBottomClearance` (used by `HomeView` and `TrendsView` to clear the raised capture circle) move with it. **General lesson worth carrying: an artboard's bottom padding is never a safe-area inset - porting it verbatim double-counts every time.** | L4: the existing "last row clears the tab bar" assertion still holds; a **device without a home indicator renders the artboard's exact 28 pt** and one with an indicator renders the floor, asserted against injected insets rather than a runtime measurement; screenshots in **both themes** (the defect was reported on the light shell) and EN+RU |

One gate: `AppStore.resetForTestsOncePerLaunch()` performs the wipe at most once per process; all five call sites (Home, RecentlyDeleted, TankLevel, TireSet, Reminder seeds) use it; the three per-type flags are gone. (2) The capture script's `en` branch passed no language arguments, so a preceding `ru` launch's `-AppleLanguages "(ru)"` persisted and `en` captures rendered Russian (P1.4-home, P3.2-parts-shelf). The `en` branch now passes `-AppleLanguages "(en)" -AppleLocale en_US` explicitly. Re-captured; the two known-bad files verified by size and re-run. | Seeds intact (no seed weakened or deleted, idempotence preserved); `swift test` 0 at 529+; `xcodebuild test` 0 at 108; `swiftlint` 0; EN captures no longer follow RU into Russian |

| **[x]** P4.10 | **LLM gateway server: `POST /extract`.** The endpoint is specified in `API.md` and its quota table (`llm_usage`) was migrated in P0.9, but **nothing builds the server that calls the model** - P6.3 is the client and assumes this exists. After the P2.8 cut this is the **only** model-assisted extraction path in the product, and the only one available to Russian receipts. Server-side provider key (hard rule 11 - an IPA is a zip), per-user quota from `llm_usage` returning the documented `402`/`429`, the 4 MB envelope cap, images processed **transiently and never written to disk or blob storage**, and a response that is exactly `ExtractionMeta` fields with per-field confidence. The server returns **field values, never domain judgements** - it does not decide mixedness, duplicates, or whether a fill is plausible (hard rule 9). **Device-side budget, normative and owned by `API.md` -> "The device's side of `/extract`":** the image is **downscaled and JPEG-compressed on device before upload**, and the device gives the round trip **3 seconds per attempt** before it stops making the user wait. On expiry the user proceeds with the locally recognized data and a message that names that next step (hard rule 7); a late answer may **only fill fields that are still blank and untouched**, marked as a suggestion (hard rule 13), and **never** changes anything after save (F4). | L2: `402` with quota exhausted and `429` per-period, both with the client falling back rather than erroring; oversize body rejected at the envelope, not the model; a request whose image is never persisted - assert the temp path and the blob bucket are both untouched after a successful call; **hard rule 12: no log line carries the image, the extracted values, or the prompt**. L1 (client): the 3 s budget fires deterministically against an injected slow transport and the sheet stays usable throughout; a late response fills a blank field and **cannot** overwrite a touched one; nothing arrives after save. **L5: compression must not cost accuracy** - re-score the receipt corpus through the compression step with the existing `AccuracyRatchetTests` scorer; hits may not fall below the recorded high-water mark, which is what stops "compress harder" from silently becoming "read worse" |
| **[x]** P4.11 | **`Date` does not round-trip exactly through persistence.** `Records.swift` stores every date as `timeIntervalSince1970`; adding the 978307200 s epoch offset shifts the fractional bits out of the Double mantissa, so a record read straight back after a write differs from what was written by **one ulp (~1.2e-7 s)** on `createdAt` / `updatedAt`. Found by P3.1a's round-trip test - the first test in the repo to assert **whole-record** equality rather than field-by-field, which is why it went unnoticed through P0.4. Measured severity: the drift is **idempotent** (a second round trip is stable), so sync's `updatedAt` LWW ordering is not at risk today - but the invariant "a record read back equals the record written" is one that sync, backup round-trips and the S1-S8 scenarios all lean on, and it is currently false. Fix is to store `timeIntervalSinceReferenceDate` (an exact Double round trip), which is a **column format change and needs a migration** - hence a task, not a patch | L1: a record of every entity type read straight back after a write equals what was written, whole-record and with no normalization; the existing sub-millisecond tolerance in `ServiceEntryTests` is **deleted** rather than loosened; migration applies forward over a seeded database and the migrated values are equal to the originals to the second |
| **[x]** P4.12 | **Corpus A/B against the cloud vision model, before P4.10 is designed.** Run 2026-08-26, full sweep (61 images, 0 errors), scored LLM-vs-rules with the one shared `CorpusScorer` and tolerance. **Result per class (rules -> LLM): receipts 46/96 -> 84/96, pump 1/46 -> 31/46, fiscal 1/3 -> 2/3, screenshots 7/24 -> 22/24.** Median latency 6.5-8.3 s, all above the 3 s device budget. The two silent failure modes are committed and asserted as named cases: `receipt-035` is the volume/price swap (70.44 read as litres) and `pump-009` is the decimal-separator shift (40.00 read as 400.0) - **both pass the arithmetic cross-check**. The probe's shift on `pump-005` did not reproduce: that fixture read exactly, three runs in a row, and `pump-009` flipped between correct and shifted across runs - the model is stochastic. **Recommendation (docs/EXTRACTION.md): the gateway cross-checks and suggests, never trusts** - a confident swap is worse than the parser's nil (hard rule 13), and the cross-check is scale- and swap-blind | L5: every class scored LLM-vs-rules with the same scorer and tolerance (offline, against committed `vision-ab/*.json`, no model call in CI); latency per image; the swap and decimal-shift cases asserted explicitly as known failure modes; a written recommendation in `docs/EXTRACTION.md`. `high-water.json` unchanged |
| **[x]** P4.13 | **PaddleOCR as a third extraction arm: embed it in Docker, measure it against the other two.** P4.12 measured the cloud VLM (receipts 84/96, pump 31/46, screenshots 22/24) but found its failures **silent** (swaps and scale shifts that pass the arithmetic cross-check), its output **stochastic**, and **every class median latency above the normative 3 s device budget** (pump peaks at 40 s). PaddleOCR is worth measuring because it attacks three of those four at once: self-hosted, so **no per-call cost and no third-party image sharing**, and plausibly far faster. **Russian is supported** (`cyrillic_PP-OCRv5_mobile_rec`; PaddleOCR-VL covers 109 languages) - the gap that killed the Foundation Models tier. Runs as a **container** (plain `docker run`, never compose) with ASP.NET reaching it behind an injected seam, exactly like `IBlobStorage` / `IApnsClient` / `IRateFeed`. **Two arms, because together they answer what neither answers alone**: **(A)** PP-OCRv5 Cyrillic recognition -> the EXISTING `FuelExtractor`, isolating recognition - if this scores about the same as Vision+parser it **confirms** `docs/EXTRACTION.md`'s central claim that interpretation is the bottleneck; if it scores far better, **that claim is wrong and the doc must be corrected in the same change**; **(B)** PaddleOCR-VL -> fields directly, competing with the DeepSeek arm on accuracy, latency, cost and determinism. Gateway-only: hard rule 1 means this can never be the primary path - on-device Vision stays tier 0. **Measured 2026-08-26:** Arm A (server det + `cyrillic_PP-OCRv5_mobile_rec`) -> `FuelExtractor` scored receipts **29/96** (rules 46/96, DeepSeek 84/96), pump **2/46** (1/46, 31/46), fiscal **1/3** (1/3, 2/3), screenshots **7/24** (7/24, 22/24) - **not** far better, so the interpretation-not-recognition split is **not falsified**, but the real finding is that the parser is **coupled to Vision's output**: PaddleOCR's detector merges `1,869 EUR/L` into one line (Vision splits it), and the Cyrillic-only reader misreads `ИТОГ` as `НТОГ`. PaddleOCR's OCR is **byte-identical across 3 runs**, but the sweep exposed a **pre-existing parser** non-determinism (`FuelExtractor.modal` ties two total candidates and breaks the tie via `Dictionary(grouping:)` iteration order) that flips `receipt-021`/`-026`/`-029` between runs (receipt score 29-30/96). Median latency 4.5-8.4 s, max 21.0 s - **above the 3 s budget in every class** even self-hosted. **Arm B (PaddleOCR-VL-0.9B) is blocked**: `paddlex` calls `is_bfloat16_supported()` with no place argument (raises on paddlepaddle 3.2.2 aarch64); forcing float32 OOM-kills the 7.6 GB Docker VM (exit 137). **Conclusion (docs/EXTRACTION.md): the ops cost is not justified** - Arm A is worse than on-device Vision, self-hosting buys no latency back without a GPU, and Arm B cannot run on modest hardware; PaddleOCR does not earn the fallback slot | L5: both arms scored with the **existing `CorpusScorer` at the same tolerance** (P4.12) - a second, subtly different comparison invalidates everything; per-class hits/total beside the rules and DeepSeek arms; **latency median and max per class against the 3 s budget**; **determinism measured, not assumed** - every image run 3x with variance reported, because the DeepSeek arm flipped `pump-009` between correct and shifted on re-run; scoring runs **offline against committed result files** so CI never needs the container; `high-water.json` unchanged. **Vacuous traps**: reporting only aggregate percentages (two silent failures matter more than a higher average); running each image once and calling it deterministic; scoring arm A against a different tolerance than the rules arm it exists to be compared with |

Measurement (median 6.5-8.3 s, max 40 s) is therefore not a contradiction; it is the reason the rule is written that way. While iOS Low Power Mode is on, the app postpones **opportunistic** work - launch/foreground/timer sync cycles, attachment upload and prefetch, the rate pack refresh, the catalog pack fetch - and changes nothing else. **The load-bearing distinction is background vs user-initiated**: a save, and any sync/restore/export/retry the user actually tapped, must run exactly as before, because a silently cancelled action the user is watching has no next step (hard rule 7) and reads as a hang. Resumes on `NSProcessInfoPowerStateDidChange`, not at next launch. Surfaces **only** as a reason on S7's existing passive status row ("Waiting to sync - 5 changes - Low Power Mode is on") - reassurance, never a warning: no amber, no badge, no toast, no modal (hard rule 8). L1: resume fires on the power-state change, not only at launch; the deferred queue then drains. L1: a save, an edit and a delete are unaffected while the mode is on (hard rule 1 - no screen is ever sync-gated). L1: blob upload/prefetch defers **without** losing the pending record. L4: the status row shows the reason in EN+RU and is **not** amber; it vanishes when the mode ends. **Vacuous traps**: a gate consulted but not awaited; asserting only that the mode was read; testing the defer path without the run path (an implementation that always defers passes); a test that reads the real `ProcessInfo` and therefore only passes on the machine that ran it |

**`api.tankbook.live` does not answer yet, and that is intentional** (product owner, 2026-08-28). nginx proxies it and returns
**502** because no backend is listening on the upstream port. Postponed deliberately, not broken: the app is local-first, so an
unreachable API costs sync, cloud extract and import parse and **nothing else** - every screen keeps working offline (hard rule 1).
Two consequences worth knowing while it stays down: the config guardrail's `GET /health` probe cannot promote a candidate
`apiBaseUrl`, so the bundled default stands; and P6.18's update requirement resolves `.none`, because a config document that never
arrives carries no `appUpdate` - which is the fail-open rule behaving exactly as designed.

## PJ · Journeys review (2026-08-29)

*Source: `docs/JOURNEYS.md` reviewed stage by stage against the tree at `93d2619` by four
read-only passes (brief `agents/briefs/REVIEW-JOURNEYS.md`); every bug-level claim below was
re-verified by hand before it was written. Gaps already tracked elsewhere are cited, not re-filed:
PR.2 (sign-out revoke), PR.7 (auto-retry), PR.13 (offline vs server-down), PR.14 (S1 row + S7
toast - its deliverable should also name Recently deleted's "Overwritten by sync" section and
Compare, both fixtures), PR.22 (LLM quota note), PR.30 (reminder hours), P2.6/P2.7/P5.5b/P6.9
open halves. J6 (EV charge) and J12 (second driver) are v1.x/v2 per `VISION.md` and are not
filed, except for the one live defect J6 leaves in v1 (PJ.12).*

*Five ticked rows claim more than the code does; PJ.47 footnotes them: **P1.1** (Welcome root
and its three edges were never built), **P2.2/P2.3** (parser ported, sheet takes a prefill - the
seam camera -> OCR -> prefill -> sheet was never a task), **P3.4** (Reminders has no production
entry point), **P4.4** (wrong-provider detection exists, the entry that arms it does not),
**P5.2** (S8 backfill runs only under a DEBUG flag), **P6.3** (only the timeout branch of F4
renders).*

*The shape of the finding, in one sentence: the Confirm sheet, the engines and the import review
are built and L4-covered, but **the scan door is painted on** - no camera or Photos image ever
becomes a `ConfirmPrefill` outside launch-argument seeds - and four screens that the journeys
route through (Welcome, Reminders, the S5 notice, the post-sign-in push) exist only behind
`-force*` fixtures.*

| ID | Task | Checks (done when...) |
|---|---|---|

## RV · Reviewer bugs (first TestFlight build, 2026-09-02/03)

*Found by running build **1.0.0+466** on a real device against the live backend, and by reading
production logs. Every row here is something no test caught, because each sits in a seam the suites
do not span: a client/server contract, a UIKit lifetime, a tier default, or a screen nobody drove on
hardware. `RV.1`-`RV.5`, `RV.7`-`RV.9` and `RV.11`-`RV.16` are fixed; `RV.10`, `RV.17`-`RV.20`, `RV.22`-`RV.26` and `RV.28`-`RV.35` are open.*

| # | Bug | Check |
|---|---|---|
| **[x]** RV.1 | **The rate pack 400'd on every launch.** `RateStore` asked for a **two-year** window; the server caps at `Rates:MaxPackDays` **400**, so every refresh was rejected - five in one session, and it would have been every launch forever. A number in two places that disagreed (`PRACTICES.md`). Invisible because the client tests use a `RateFetcher` double that accepts any range and the server tests choose their own: **both sides tested, the contract between them not** | The client's requested span is recorded and compared against the server cap; mutation-checked - restoring the two-year window fails with `730 <= 400` |
| **[x]** RV.2 | **Choosing a photo from the library did nothing** - the app stayed on the capture screen. `PHPickerViewControllerDelegate` set `isPresented = false` first, which tears down the representable and releases the `Coordinator`, so the `[weak self]` in the async `loadObject` callback was already nil and the pick was dropped. A **race**, which is why review and the simulator missed it: a small local image can beat the teardown, an iCloud photo that must download never does | `self` is captured strongly (a `@MainActor` class is `Sendable`), so delivery no longer depends on how long the load takes. Needs a device re-test with an iCloud photo |
| **[x]** RV.3 | **The per-request log line buried everything else.** One `http.request` at Information per call, several per screen, hiding `auth.session`, `sync.push`, `blob.commit` | Success is `Debug`, 4xx `Information`, 5xx `Warning`, plus a readable summary. Re-levelled, not removed: a test pins that the successful line still appears at `Debug` |
| **[x]** RV.4 | **DONE 2026-09-03, product owner: the free tier gets cloud extract in v1.** The allowance was 0, so `POST /extract` answered 402 to every user - correct against `API.md`'s "LLM gateway (Pro)", except Pro is cut from v1 (`P6.16`), so the gate applied to nobody and refused everyone while the served config document promised `llmQuota.cloudFallback: 50`. Both halves were individually right and the pair was a lie. Free now gets exactly the advertised 50/day, from a named constant, and it stays configuration (`LlmGateway__TierRequestsPerPeriod__free`) so the number can move without a deploy. The alternative was capture on on-device Vision alone - **38.3%** on receipts against the cloud model's 84/96 (`P4.12`) | 3 L1 tests: free gets the advertised number, free is entitled **at all** (asserted separately - an equality check against a zero constant would still pass at zero), and an unknown tier is still refused. Mutation-checked: restoring 0 fails two of the three |
| **[x]** RV.5 | **DONE 2026-09-03. No preview or confirmation after a capture.** A shot is taken and the flow moves on with nothing shown; the user cannot see what was captured, accept it, or re-take. Requested by the product owner from the device walk. Touches `JOURNEYS.md` (J1/J3), `SCREENMAP.md` (a screen between capture and Confirm) and `ERRORS.md` (the re-take affordance), so the docs move with it - and hard rule 15 applies: re-take and "type it instead" are peers, not a failure branch | DONE: `CaptureReviewView` is a full-screen cover between both capture doors and the Confirm sheet - the image fitted (never cropped), **Use this** (which is where the recognition pipeline now runs, so a re-take costs no OCR), **Re-take** (back to the live camera, nothing kept) and **Type it** as its equal-width neighbour. `CaptureReviewUITests` (4 tests, run): the shot is shown and both verdicts are reachable, Type it is a peer by frame geometry and opens the manual form, Use this reaches Confirm with the pipeline's real litres, and Re-take returns to the camera and **does not** present Confirm. `JOURNEYS.md` (J1, J3, J3b), `SCREENMAP.md` (graph, inventory, dead-end audit, a section for the step) and `ERRORS.md` (Capture) moved with it; screenshots EN and RU |
| **[x]** RV.6 (2026-09-04, verified by orchestrator: swift build 0, `swift test` **1250/117**, localization gate 0, app build 0, zero lint errors outside the sibling worktree; mutation-checked personally - restoring the unconditional refetch fails 3 tests including the round-trip count assertion, restored byte-identical. Commit `48d9313`. **INVESTIGATED 2026-09-04, read-only pro agent. Verdict: PARTIALLY fixed - the automatic burst is gone, a navigation shape remains, and TWO claims in this row's own text are wrong.** **(1) RV.18 fixed the automatic re-poll**, which was the most probable source of the production log: pre-RV.18 every `scenePhase == .active` transition ran a full opportunistic cycle -> `runSync` -> `refresh()` -> a devices fetch, and RV.18 measured launch alone firing **twice ~0.6 s apart**. `runOpportunisticSync` now returns early inside 30 s, so that shape is impossible. **(2) A different shape still reproduces, and it is not a poll at all**: `AppSync.refresh()` re-fetches the device count **unconditionally on every call** (`AppSync.swift:312-315`; `fetchedDeviceCount` is set to nil and re-fetched, never reused), and `SettingsView`'s `.task` calls `refresh()` on **every appearance** - ungated. So one "check my devices" round trip costs **three** fetches: Settings appears (#1), push AccountDevices (#2, `didLoad`-guarded so once per push), pop back and Settings `.task` re-runs (#3). Gear -> account -> back -> account is four real GETs in well under 14 s with no sync cycle involved. **There is no de-duplication anywhere**: no in-flight guard, no minimum interval, and RV.18's 30 s gate covers only `runOpportunisticSync`, not this path. **CORRECTION 1 - this row's premise is factually wrong**: *"the per-IP limits key on it"* - there is **no rate limit on `GET /account/devices`**. `Program.cs:584` maps it with no `.RequireRateLimiting` while six neighbouring groups have one, and `API.md`'s table lists limits only for `auth/session`, `auth/refresh`, `import/parse`, `extract`, `sync/push`, `blobs/begin` and `feedback`. The real cost is battery, plus the future risk if a limit is ever attached to this un-throttled GET. **CORRECTION 2 - RV.22 did NOT worsen it**, which was the orchestrator's stated suspicion when briefing: `SyncStateChip` reads only the computed `surfaceState` plus stored counts, never calls `refresh()` or `devices()`, and is gated to the active tab root. **Also noted**: RV.26's refresher can double a devices GET on a 401 (the screen's client wires it, the count path deliberately does not) - real, but not the polling shape.**) | **`GET /v1/account/devices` is polled repeatedly** - four calls in 14 seconds (21:07:23, :28, :30, :37), then more. Looks like a screen refetching on every appearance rather than on change. Cheap per call, but it is the shape that becomes a battery and rate-limit problem, and the per-IP limits key on it **ACCEPTANCE CORRECTED 2026-09-04 by the investigation**: "fetch on appear and on an explicit refresh" is what `AccountDevicesView` **already does** (`didLoad`-guarded, one fetch per push) - so the original acceptance would have passed against the bug. The residual repetition is the **sync surface's count fetch piggybacking on every `refresh()`**, which the row never named. The fix is there: either reuse `fetchedDeviceCount` and re-fetch only when nil (clearing it explicitly on sign-in, sign-out and after a revoke/delete), or drop the count fetch from `refresh()` entirely and let the AccountDevices screen be the sole fetcher - collapsing the round trip from three fetches to one. **The test must assert a REQUEST COUNT over a recording transport** - `GET /v1/account/devices` fires <= 1 across `Settings -> AccountDevices -> pop -> AccountDevices` - never "the screen loaded", which passes against the bug. The seeded `AccountStubTransport`/`SeededLaunchTransport` already serve this path, so the assertion is cheap | The device list is fetched on appear and on an explicit refresh, not on every re-render; L4 asserts the call count across a navigation cycle |
| **[x]** RV.7 | **DONE 2026-09-03.** `RatesHostedService` now runs one pass BEFORE entering the timer loop. `PeriodicTimer.WaitForNextTickAsync` waits a full interval before its first tick, so with the 6-hour default there was no pass for six hours after a start - and every deploy restarts the process and resets that clock, so deploying more often than six-hourly meant it **never ran**. That is what production showed: `/v1/rates` served a correctly-shaped, permanently empty pack while answering 200, so nothing looked wrong. The pass is idempotent - the job's own comment calls the interval "a liveness knob, not a correctness one" - so one extra run at boot costs a query and removes the dependence on uptime | L2 drives `StartAsync` with a 24-hour interval, so no tick can arrive, and asserts the feed is reached. **The first version of this test was VACUOUS** - it called `RunOnePassAsync` directly, so deleting the startup call from `ExecuteAsync` still passed; it now drives the real entry point and the mutation fails it |
| **[x]** RV.8 | **DONE 2026-09-03. A ten-second wait with nothing on screen.** Production measured `llm.extract DurationMs=10218` on a real receipt; the Confirm sheet showed **nothing at all** for the first 3 s and a **motionless hourglass** for the remaining 7, which reads as a finished statement rather than as work in progress. The gateway session already knew - `phase` has carried `.running` since P6.3 - but only `.budgetExpired` was ever rendered, so the state that covers the whole wait was the one state with no view. Reported by the product owner from the device walk | `GatewayReadingBanner` renders `.running` and `.budgetExpired` as one banner in two states (same shape, same place, different words), both carrying the small `inkSoft` `ProgressView` the rest of the app uses, degrading to the static hourglass under Reduce Motion. L4 asserts the running banner is on screen BEFORE the 3 s budget and the timeout banner after; screenshots EN and RU |
| **[x]** RV.9 | **DONE 2026-09-03. An attachment could not be opened.** The receipt strip rendered a 44x56 thumbnail that was **decorative by construction** - `AttachmentPhotoChip`'s own doc comment said so - so no tap target in the app opened a receipt at full size and there was no zoom. The photo the entry exists to evidence could only be squinted at. All-or-nothing. **(d) Write nothing at first launch.** No stored preference means "follow the system", so a user who later changes their phone's language sees the app follow; storing the system value at first launch would silently freeze it. Seeding now runs once (`didSeed`) while the **data reloads on every call**. With that, the two known halves landed: Garage gained `.onChange(of: selection.selectedID)` and `.onChange(of: toastCenter.revision)`, mirroring Home and Trends, and `AddVehicleView.save()` now calls `noteEntryChanged()` so the add path raises a signal at all. Verified by the orchestrator: swiftlint 0 errors, `swift build` 0, `GarageUITests` **7 tests 0 failures**, and mutation-checked personally - removing the add-path signal fails the regression test. `SCREENMAP.md` gained a "tab roots reload on signals, not on reappearance" convention, which is the durable part: the tab roots stay mounted across a switch, so `.task` fires once and never again |

L2: with history present for a quote and a gap in the middle, the backfill fills exactly the gap and nothing else; with no history at all, it establishes the window. **Then verify the reported entry actually converts** - a green test with that 2 Sep fill still pending means the chain is not closed  **SHAPE DECIDED 2026-09-03 (product owner): the backfill is DEMAND-DRIVEN, not a fixed window.** The trigger is *"we hold an entry whose `rateDate` has no rate"*, which covers the case that made a fixed window wrong: **import**. `POST /import/parse` lets a user bring in years of MFM history in one action (`docs/API.md`, the rule-9 exception), and those entries can be dated well outside any rolling window - a 400-day ceiling would leave everything older permanently pending, which is the bug this row exists for, reintroduced one day later. So: **the set of dates to fetch is derived from the data, not from the calendar.** Consequences to design for. **(a) The device knows which dates are missing; the server does not** - it holds no entries (hard rule 9), so the *device* must ask, either by requesting a rate for a date it lacks or by widening its pack request. Do not invent a server-side domain query to find them; that is exactly what rule 9 forbids. **(b) An import can request hundreds of distinct dates at once** - bound the burst, dedupe, and make it resumable; a user importing five years must not fire 1800 upstream requests. **(c) The upstream has no rate for a weekend or a holiday**, and asking again every launch would be a permanent retry loop - a date answered "nothing published" must be recorded as answered, distinct from "not yet asked". **(d) Backfilled rows are `published` when they come from a real document for that date and `:carried-forward` only when they do not** - the distinction RV.20 established.  **GAP RULE DECIDED 2026-09-03 (product owner): a date with no published rate takes the most recent earlier known rate, within a bounded window.** This is the right shape and it dissolves the "answered-empty vs not-yet-asked" bookkeeping entirely - a weekend resolves from Friday and is finished, so nothing retries. Look **backwards only**: the rate in force on the day of the fill is one that already existed; a later rate did not. The row is stored `:carried-forward`, never `published` (RV.20). **The window was proposed as 7 days and MEASURED AGAINST THE LIVE FEED as too short.** CBR does not publish across the Russian New Year: asking `date_req` for 01, 05, 08, 09, 10 and 12 January 2026 all return the document dated **31.12.2025**, and the first January document is **13.01.2026** - a **13-day gap**. A 7-day window would leave 07-12 January permanently unresolved, in **RUB**, which is a home currency for this app. **Use 14 days** (13 measured, one day of margin) and pin the January window as a test fixture - the holiday document is already captured at `backend/tests/.../Fixtures/cbr-xml-daily-2026-01-04-holiday-windows1251.xml`. **The bound is not decoration**: without it an imported entry predating all rate history would silently take some unrelated rate rather than staying honestly pending. **And carrying back must not hide a broken feed** - RV.15 went unnoticed for weeks because nothing downstream looked wrong; `SourcesFailed` must still be reported even while gaps are being filled. |

Note the `sha256` is content-addressed, so an identical image uploaded again would dedupe to the same hash: a weak re-identification path, worth a sentence in the doc rather than a redesign. L2: a successful call writes exactly one row with every field populated; a **failed** call writes one too, with the error category - a table that only records successes cannot answer the question it exists for. Assert deletion removes the blob and the bodies while the row and its ledger fields survive - asserting only that the row is still there passes against a deletion that purged nothing. | **[x]** RV.36 | **A carried-forward placeholder permanently displaces the real published rate for that day.** Found while resolving RV.30. `RateRepository.UpsertAsync` is `ON CONFLICT (date, base, quote) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL DO NOTHING` - the append-only guarantee. It does not distinguish a **published** row from a `:carried-forward` one, so once a placeholder occupies a slot, the genuine rate published later that same day **can never take its place**. **This is systematic, not occasional**: ECB publishes ~16:00 CET, the job runs every 6 h from process start, and any deploy or restart in the morning makes the day's first pass carry forward. The 2026-09-03 data is the proof - **29 ECB quotes for that date are all `ecb:carried-forward`**, i.e. 09-02's rates standing in for 09-03, and no later pass can correct them. L2: a carried row is replaced by a later published row for the same date/base/quote; a published row is **not** replaced by another published row; a carried row is not replaced by another carried row. Mutation-checked - restoring the blanket `DO NOTHING` must fail the first  **DONE 2026-09-03.** `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE ... The append-only guarantee for real data is untouched: published never overwrites published, carried never overwrites carried. `RateSources` gained the suffix as the single source of truth rather than a literal at the call site. Verified by the orchestrator: `dotnet build` 0, `dotnet format` 0, **339 tests 0 skipped** (336 before), and mutation-checked personally - restoring the blanket `DO NOTHING` fails **1 of 3** tests, the supersede case, while **both negatives still pass**, which is the discrimination that proves they guard the append-only rule rather than the new behaviour. No stored `money` value is rewritten (hard rule 3: snapshots immutable) - an entry already converted at the carried rate keeps it |

RV.38's inbox is therefore honest but lossy: an answer only arrives if the app happened to stay alive. **Three cases, and they do NOT get the same answer.** **(a) Reminders: no backend, ever.** They are scheduled locally and fire locally; routing them through a server would break hard rule 1 for a feature that never needed the network. **(b) A late extraction the device never received: a narrow backend role is justified** - see the shape below. **(c) Cross-device inbox state** (dismiss on one phone, it clears on the other): that is ordinary sync of a synced entity, already a named rule-1 exception, and it is **out of scope here** - decide it separately or the delivery mechanism quietly becomes a sync surface | **THE SHAPE, DECIDED 2026-09-04: an opaque per-device OUTBOX, not a read over the audit ledger.** The obvious implementation - let the device read its answer back from `llm_calls` - is the one thing that must not happen: RV.33's rule-9 amendment says the ledger is *"written by the gateway and read by no endpoint"*, and turning the audit record into a delivery channel reverses that days after it was written. Instead the gateway, when it **cannot** deliver (client gone, 499/timeout), enqueues the result into a small per-device outbox the device drains on next launch. Why that is rule-9 clean and the ledger read is not: the outbox row is **opaque bytes addressed to a device** - the server never reads a field, never queries by meaning, and offers no search or stats; it is the same shape as `GET /blobs/{sha256}`, retrieve-what-you-are-entitled-to. **It is still a third place storing user content, and RV.33's amendment explicitly says that needs its own written decision** - so `CLAUDE.md` rule 9 gains one, in the same commit. **Bounds, all matching what already exists**: retention **30 days** (the tombstone/undo window, `/import/parse`'s file and the ledger all use it); **drain-and-ack** so a collected row is deleted rather than re-delivered forever; **requires an account** (so does `/extract`); **purged by `DELETE /account`** like every other content store; and **nothing is logged but shape** - counts, ids, outcome codes, never a payload (hard rule 12). **The nudge is silent APNs**, which `docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md` already defines - the outbox is what makes the wake worth doing. **Hard rule 1 is untouched**: no screen is gated on it, a guest never has one, and an undelivered answer costs a suggestion, never an entry |

So `checkmark.icloud` turned forever. **Fix:** branch on the spinning state so the moving and still glyphs have **different view identities** - the animating view is torn down when the sync ends and its animation goes with it. Both branches are the same `Image` at the same font, so the baseline and header height RV.22 fixed stay fixed | **No test can catch this, and that is worth recording rather than papering over.** XCUITest asserts behaviour and never appearance; a screenshot is a still frame and cannot show that something is still moving. The guard is structural: the branch makes the bug unexpressible, and the comment at the call site says why, so a future "simplification" back to one view with a conditional animation reintroduces it knowingly. `SyncChipUITests` 12/12 still green (they pin the state's label, which was always correct - the label said "Synced" the whole time it was spinning, which is precisely why they missed it) |

Commit `00db099`.) | **The rate backfill queue never drains: 50 dates processed every 5 minutes, forever, accomplishing nothing.** From production logs 2026-09-04, and it is the clearest signal in them - **20 consecutive passes** from 06:21 to 07:56 reading `rates.backfill Processed=50 Published=0 CarriedForward=0 SourcesFailed=0`. Fifty is exactly `BackfillBatchSize`, so a full batch is drained every pass and **nothing is published, nothing carried, nothing even fails** - and it continues across long windows with no device traffic at all (06:56-07:31 has no sync, no pack, nothing). **`SettleAsync` is called unconditionally** on every date in the batch (`RateBackfillService.cs:101-104`) and deletes by `(base, date)`, so rows ARE being removed - which means they are being **put back**. **Prime suspect, verified as a predicate mismatch in the code**: `RecordRequestAsync` enqueues a date when `!sources.IsSubsetOf(families)` - i.e. when **any** feed family lacks a row - while `BackfillDatesAsync` skips a feed when `families.Contains(feed.Source)` - i.e. it only fetches from feeds that have **no** row. A date that one feed covers and another structurally never will (a feed with no data for that day, a quote outside its remit) therefore satisfies "enqueue me" forever while the backfill correctly concludes there is nothing to fetch. Enqueue -> no-op -> settle -> re-enqueue, permanently. `SourcesFailed=0` says no upstream call is even being made, which fits: the work is skipped, not failed. **Cost today is DB churn rather than money** - no upstream requests are burned - but it is a permanently hot loop, it makes the `rates.backfill` line useless as a health signal (it looks identical whether the system is healthy or wedged), and the next person to read it will assume the backfill is working | **The two predicates must agree on granularity.** Decide whether "covered" means *every feed family has a row* or *this date has a usable rate*, and apply the SAME test on both sides - or a date that is legitimately unfillable will always look pending to one and finished to the other. **A date that cannot be filled must be recorded as ANSWERED, not merely settled**, so it is never re-enqueued: RV.32's own row already named this (*"a date answered 'nothing published' must be recorded as answered, distinct from 'not yet asked'"*) and the carry-back rule was thought to have dissolved it - it did not, for dates outside the 14-day window. **Confirm the mechanism with one query before building**, exactly as RV.30/RV.36 were settled: `SELECT base, count(*), min(date), max(date) FROM rate_backfill GROUP BY base` on two passes five minutes apart - if the same rows persist, it is re-enqueue; if the ids churn, it is something else and this diagnosis is wrong. L2: a date no feed can fill is processed **once** and does not reappear in the next pass - assert the queue is EMPTY on the second pass, not that the first pass succeeded. Mutation-check that assertion |

EE100305317` / `Terminal` / `Kood T07` - *"there are all the data, but for a user and for the app important is only entry data. The data must be stripped, filtered and left only meaningful. That's the goal of the local OCR."* **Verified, and the display is a symptom of a storage decision.** `Attachment` (`Entities.swift`) carries exactly `ocrText: String?` and `extractedTimestamp: Date?` - **the raw lines and a clock, and nothing else**. There is no field for what the parse concluded. So `AttachmentRecognisedView` (RV.17) renders `ocrText` because raw text is *the only thing that exists*; it is not a presentation bug that a nicer view would fix. **The assignment is computed and then thrown away.** The local stack is real and substantial - `FuelExtractor`, `ExtractionAssembler`, `NumberScanner`, `CrossCheck`, `DigitRepair`, `FuelKindNormalizer`, `CurrencyDetection` in `TankbookCore/Extraction/` - and it does decide that `51,71` is the total, `1,919 EUR/L` the unit price and `26,94` the volume. That `FuelExtraction` flows into the Confirm form as a pre-fill and is then discarded: the ENTRY keeps the resulting values, the ATTACHMENT keeps no record of what was read as what. **So the product owner's framing is right about the consequence and half-right about the cause**: local extraction exists, it is simply not *kept* - and separately it succeeds on only **38.3%** of receipts against the cloud's **84/96** (P4.12), which is why the gateway carries the load. Fixing the storage does not fix the accuracy, and the row must not pretend it does. **A privacy point rides along**: the raw dump carries the merchant's registration and VAT numbers (`Reg.kood`, `KMKR nr.`) and terminal ids - data the app has no use for, stored on device and pushed through sync. Keeping only the assigned fields is data minimisation, not just tidiness (`docs/SECURITY.md`) | **Persist the assignment on the attachment**, so the app remembers what each number MEANT: total, unit price, volume, date, fuel kind, station, currency - each with its confidence, which `FuelExtraction` already carries. `docs/SCHEMA.md` gains the field and `docs/SYNC.md` the payload change; this is a schema evolution, so it is a data change with a declarative transform, never a backend deploy (hard rule 9). **Then the viewer shows MEANING, and the raw lines stop being the headline.** Keep the raw text - it is what lets a bad parse be re-examined, and `docs/EXTRACTION.md`'s named failure modes depend on it - but demote it behind a disclosure rather than deleting it, and **say which you chose and why**. **Decide explicitly whether the stored raw text is trimmed** on the privacy ground above; deleting evidence of a misread to save bytes is a real cost, so this is a decision, not an optimisation. **This is a hard dependency of RV.45**, which builds the yours-vs-receipt comparison: that card cannot show what the receipt said per field while the only stored artefact is a line dump - sequence them, RV.48 first, or RV.45 will invent its own storage. **Hard rule 13 is untouched**: persisting what was read changes nothing about who decides - the values are still suggestions the user accepts per field. L1: an attachment written from a real corpus fixture carries the assigned fields with the values the extractor produced, and an attachment whose parse assigned nothing carries no fields rather than empty strings (absent, never a blank). L4: the viewer's recognised page shows the assigned values, and a receipt with nothing assigned says so instead of rendering an empty card. Use the corpus fixtures, not a hand-typed string - `Spike/ReceiptSpike/fixtures` is the point of having them. EN + RU |
| **[x]** RV.47 (2026-09-04, verified by orchestrator by captured exit code: swift build 0, swiftlint 0 errors in the touched files, localization gate 0, app build 0, `RV47FieldRowFocusUITests` **6/6** - assertions are behavioural (label tap against a field emptied first, gap tap, picker row asserted NOT to focus, row hit height measured **53.67 pt**). Mutation-checked personally: removing the tap gesture fails 3 of 6; restored byte-identical. Empty-field visible extent considered and declined with reasons, so no DESIGN.md change. Commit `78939d8`.) | **Tapping a field's LABEL does nothing - the only tap target is the input itself, which is invisible and nearly zero-width when empty.** Reported by the product owner 2026-09-04: *"a user can click on a field name, such as 'odometer', and expects that the input field will be activated. Today the user must click on an empty field, which is not obvious."* **Verified, and the cause is three things compounding in the SHARED row component** (`FieldRow`, `AddVehicleComponents.swift:78`): **(1)** `FieldRow` is an inert `HStack` - label `Text`, `Spacer`, value - with **no `contentShape`, no tap gesture and no focus binding**, so the label and the wide gap beside it swallow taps; **(2)** the inputs are `TextField("", text:)` with `.multilineTextAlignment(.trailing)` and **no placeholder**, so an EMPTY field collapses to a near-zero-width hit target pinned to the right edge - far under the 44 pt floor `DESIGN.md` sets; **(3)** the `fieldUnderline` that would show the field's extent is an `overlay` on that same collapsed field **and renders only when focused or warning** - so in the empty, unfocused state there is *nothing on screen indicating where to tap*. The affordance is invisible precisely in the state where the user needs it. **This is hard rule 15, not a nicety**: typing is a peer entry path, and *"any screen that makes manual entry harder to reach than capture is a bug"* - a field the user cannot find is manual entry made harder, on the app's busiest screen. **The blast radius is the whole form layer, which is the good news**: `FieldRow` is used by **eight** files - `ManualFillUpSections`, `ManualFillUpFuelCard`, `EditEntryRows`, `EditEntryNonFillView`, `AddVehicleControls`, `VehicleDetailSections`, `VehicleFormControls`, `VehicleUnitsEditor` - so one fix lands on Confirm, Edit entry, Add car and Vehicle detail at once. **Precedent exists**: RV.10 made the whole date-row header the tap target with `.contentShape(Rectangle())` for exactly this reason | **The whole row focuses its field**, label and gap included - the `<label for>` behaviour users expect. `FieldRow` needs the focus target passed in (it is a generic value container today, so this is a real signature change across eight call sites; do it once, properly, rather than patching one screen). **Rows with no editable field must not become fake buttons** - a value row that opens a picker already has its own tap behaviour (RV.10's date row), and a read-only row must stay inert; state how you told them apart. **Consider giving the empty field a visible extent** - a persistent hairline underline, or the placeholder these fields deliberately omit - because a 44 pt row that focuses on tap still leaves the user guessing *where the value goes* until they tap. That is a `DESIGN.md` change if taken, so decide it explicitly rather than drifting. L4 asserts BEHAVIOUR, not existence: **tap the label text and assert the field is focused** (the keyboard is up and the caret is in that field), for a numeric row and a text row; tap the gap between label and value and assert the same; assert a read-only/picker row is unaffected. **Assert the row's hit height is >= 44 pt** - the accessibility floor is the measurable half of this bug. EN + RU screenshots only if a visible affordance is added |
| **[x]** RV.45 (2026-09-04, verified by orchestrator by captured exit code: `swift test` 1239/114, swiftlint 0 serious, localization gate 0 (697 keys), app build, `InboxUITests` 9/9. Mutation-checked personally - making every field apply regardless of its tick fails 5 core tests including "replaces exactly that field"; restored byte-identical. All four screenshots opened personally, RU fits. Uses the inbox item's own extraction, so it invented no storage - RV.48 remains needed for the VIEWER. Commit `2e36049`.) | **The inbox card asks the user to decide and shows them nothing to decide on - and its main action provably does nothing in the commonest case.** Required by the product owner 2026-09-04: *"worth to show to a user recognized data (volume, price, date, gas type) - what will be replaced and will it be replaced anything? The user should see what they have entered and what will be updated after recognition to make a decision."* **The presentation gap is real and the defect under it is worse.** Verified in `GatewayInboxPolicy` (RV.38/RV.44): `fillableFields` can only ever return **`.unitPrice`**, because on a SAVED `FillUp` the other fields are never blank - `volumeL`, `date`, `fuelKind` and `money` are all non-nil by construction (the doc comment says so outright). But `shouldOffer` fires on `fillableFields` **OR `hasDifference`**, and `hasDifference` compares total, volume, unit price, fuel kind, currency and date. **So the commonest item is a DISAGREEMENT, and for every one of those `merged()` changes nothing at all** - it fills only a blank price. The card then offers "Update from the receipt" as a prominent action that is a **guaranteed no-op**, with no indication of what differs. That is hard rule 7 (an action must name what it actually does) on top of the missing information, and it makes the whole ask misleading: the user is asked to choose between three options, one of which silently does nothing, on the basis of no data. **The disagreement is the single most valuable thing the scan produced** - a receipt reading 47.30 L against a typed 42.30 L is exactly the typo the camera exists to catch - and today it is computed, used to justify showing the card, and then thrown away unshown | **DECIDED 2026-09-04 (product owner): PER-FIELD, the user ticks each.** The card lists every field the receipt read - date, fuel kind, volume, unit price, total, currency - as **yours vs the receipt**, marks which differ, and lets the user choose **per field** what to take. **This is compatible with hard rule 13 and is the only option that is**: rule 13 forbids the APP overwriting a user value (*"no catalog update, sync merge, re-scan or later curation may overwrite it"*), and a per-field tick is the USER deciding, which the same rule requires be possible (*"editable at the moment it is offered and again afterwards"*). Accept-all was rejected for making one tap discard typed entries - the exact thing the pre-RV.38 drop existed to prevent. **The honesty requirements are the hard part, not the table.** (a) A field that matches is not a decision - show it as agreement or not at all, never as a choice. (b) **If nothing would change, the card must say so** and must not offer an action that does nothing. (c) The blank-fill case (price) and the overwrite case (a differing value) are different acts and must read differently - filling a blank is not replacing a value the user typed. (d) Values render per `DESIGN.md` (DIN numerals, `tabular-nums`, money amount-then-symbol with U+00A0) so a comparison lines up column-wise. L4: a card with one differing field and one blank field offers exactly two ticks; **ticking only the blank one leaves the differing field byte-identical** (assert the VALUE); ticking the differing one replaces exactly that field and nothing else (assert every other field too); with nothing to change the card says so and offers no update action. L1 in core for the per-field merge - one policy, feeding both the in-process and outbox paths (RV.44). EN + RU, and RU is where a two-column comparison overflows |

The seed now pins its fill to the start of the month it runs in instead of `daysAgo: 6`. **The agent's mutation check is the evidence**: restoring `daysAgo: 6` reproduced the original failure at `TrendsUITests.swift:129` on today's real clock (`MUTATION_UITEST=65`), and the fix restored it to green - I confirmed both in its log. It also audited EVERY `daysAgo` seed rather than only the failing one and said why each other is safe (`seedHomeFullHistory` carries an explicit `daysAgo: 0` entry guaranteeing a current-month row on any date). The added L1 pins the bug in BOTH directions - a fill on the 1st counts, a fill six days before the 1st does not - using a fixed gregorian/UTC date, so it does not repeat the two-clocks mistake of [RV.55]. Commit `dd0f196`.) | **`TrendsUITests.testSingleFillShowsOnlyTheTilesThatHaveHonestData` fails on the 1st-6th of every month.** Found by the RV.22 agent and **confirmed by the orchestrator on a clean tree** (2026-09-03, `EXIT=65` at `TrendsUITests.swift:129`). `-seedHomeSingleFill` writes its fill at `FillSpec(daysAgo: 6, ...)` (`HomeTestSeed.swift:87`), and `HomeStats.monthSpend` filters entries to the calendar month of `asOf` (`HomeStats.swift:151-152`). Today is Sep 3, so the seeded fill lands on Aug 28 - a different month - `monthSpend` returns nil, the spend tile is absent, and the first assertion fails. **The test is right and the seed is wrong**: the test's claim ("a single fill shows spend and price, but not consumption or cost/km") is a true statement about the product; the seed just picks a date that silently changes meaning depending on when the suite runs. It passes for roughly 24 days a month, which is exactly what makes it expensive - it will be blamed on whatever change happens to be in flight on the 1st. **Not caused by RV.22, RV.26 or RV.29** - `monthSpend` is untouched by all three (RV.29 changed only `lastUnitPrice`) | Pin the seed to a date that is always inside the current month - e.g. the 1st of the current month, or `daysAgo` clamped so it cannot cross the boundary - rather than a fixed offset. **Do not fix it by relaxing the assertion**: "assert the tile exists OR does not" would make the test vacuous, and the tile's presence is the product claim being tested. **Audit the other seeds for the same shape in the same change** - any `daysAgo` large enough to cross a month boundary against a month-filtered stat has this bug latent, and a grep for `daysAgo:` is the whole audit. L4: the suite passes with the simulator clock set to the 1st of a month - state how you verified it, since running it on the 20th proves nothing |
| **[x]** RV.42 (2026-09-04, verified by orchestrator by captured exit code: LanguagePreference L1 10/10, swiftlint 0 errors, localization gate 0, app build, `LanguageSettingsUITests` 5/5 including the survives-dismiss assertion; both screenshots re-shot after the colour change and opened personally, RU wraps cleanly. Commit `72798bb`. **COLOUR DECIDED 2026-09-04, product owner: the pending notice is AMBER (`warn`), not `inkSoft`.** *"There is a note below the setting, but it's blind. As it's a warning, it should be of amber color."* The current `.footnote` + `inkSoft` is what `DESIGN.md` classifies as **inert status** - placeholder glyphs, informational icons, status badges - which is precisely why it reads as decoration and gets skipped. **This is hard-rule-5 compliant, and the check matters**: amber is reserved for *attention only*, and a setting the user has changed that has NOT taken effect until they relaunch is attention by definition - there is an action outstanding. It is not an error (nothing failed, nothing is lost), so it stays a notice in `warn`, never red and never a system dialog. Apply `Theme.Palette.warn` from the token, never a literal hex, and carry an icon alongside the colour - `DESIGN.md`'s accessibility floor says colour is never the only channel, and an amber line with no glyph fails that for a colour-blind reader exactly as the old grey one failed everyone.) | **Changing the language tells you to relaunch in a notice that vanishes the moment you leave the picker.** Reported by the product owner 2026-09-03: *"after I changed the language in the setting, I wasn't asked / prompted / notified to re-launch the app to apply the settings."* **The notice exists and is unmissable-by-design in exactly the wrong way.** RV.24 shipped `L10n.languageRestartPrompt` ("Language changes the next time you open Tankbook" / "Язык изменится при следующем открытии Tankbook"), but `LanguagePickerView` renders it **only inside the picker sheet** (`LanguagePickerView.swift:99-100`), below the option list, as a `.footnote` in `inkSoft` - the quietest text style on the screen - and gates it on `@State private var pendingChange` (`:72`, set at `:152`/`:159`). **`@State` dies with the sheet**: tapping a language moves the checkmark, and the natural next action is the `Готово`/Done button in the navigation bar, which dismisses the sheet and destroys the only notice. Back in Settings the Language row shows the NEW value with nothing indicating the app is still running in the OLD language - so the user is left with a setting that visibly took effect and an app that visibly did not change, which reads as a broken switch rather than a pending one. **This is hard rule 7, not a copy tweak**: *"every error names its next step **and survives being ignored**"* - this notice is defined by not surviving being ignored. Note the constraint that makes the notice necessary is correct and must not be traded away: iOS applies `AppleLanguages` at launch, and a programmatic exit to apply a setting reads as a crash and risks App Store rejection (`ERRORS.md`, RV.24) - so the answer is a notice that persists, never a relaunch the app performs itself | The pending-relaunch state must **outlive the picker** and be visible where the user ends up. It is derived, not stored (hard rule 2 in spirit): the app is pending a relaunch exactly while the stored preference differs from `Bundle.main.preferredLocalizations.first` - a pure comparison that belongs in core beside `LanguagePreference.resolve` with its own L1 tests, and which **self-clears on the next launch** with nothing to reset. Surface it on the Settings Language row itself (the row the user just changed), and decide explicitly whether the moment of choice also deserves a confirmation rather than a caption - the product owner's words were *"asked / prompted / notified"*, and a caption is none of those. **L1: preference set to a language the bundle is not currently rendering -> pending; preference matching the running language -> not pending; no preference (follow-system) -> not pending.** **L4: choose a language, dismiss the picker, and assert the pending notice is STILL VISIBLE in Settings** - that single assertion is the whole bug, and a test that only checks the notice inside the sheet passes against today's code. Assert the notice's text, not that some element exists. Copy EN + RU as whole phrases (hard rule 10); RU runs longer and this sits on a row that already carries a value and a chevron. `ERRORS.md` (the RV.24 Settings row) moves in the same change; screenshots EN and RU of Settings showing the pending state. Mutation-check: make the pending state `@State`-scoped again and confirm the L4 assertion goes red |

## Working rules

- One task = one PR = code + its checks; the PR description quotes the task ID and its check list, ticked.
- A task discovering an undocumented behavior extends the owning doc in the same PR (CLAUDE.md rule).
- Snapshot baselines are reviewed like code; accuracy high-water marks only ratchet up.
